city-digital-twin · Newcastle study · project report

Project report - the fifteenth, on F38's arm 0

2026-09-29HEAD 6bc1848branch praneetdhoolia/f37-quality-to-fourgate PASS after regenerating two stale generated artefacts (6bc18487)

Built well and measured honestly, and further from its goal at this reading than at the last. F38's arm 0 ran to 250 across three warm-start joins and reads 1 of 12 modes inside 10 % (car +6.3 %), against 2 of 12 on F37. The two demand changes did what they were built to do - walk and bike trips shorter, the rail excess down a third - and exposed what the choice lacks: motorbike chosen at a zero constant is +466 %, and the pt submode split has no frequency term, so infrequent rail and ferry run double while frequent bus and tram fall. The code rose (10 of 30 cells at 4, up from 5; no Java method over 150 lines); the documents fell (21 contradictions, the board's hand lines stale under its own result); the host is the arm's weakest part (1 of the last 3 arms ran clean). Ratings below 4 remain in every lane; each carries the concrete change that raises it.

Method

Phase 0 gate and tree at 6bc18487; Phase 1 the three collectors; six agents in parallel, one per phase (code review of six areas, performance, history, documents/process/issues, field survey, factor ledger), each read-only except the reference library; Phase 8 merged by a script from the lanes' JSON; ten file:line citations and five sources searched this pass re-verified by the synthesiser (§8.7). Ratings 1-5 per dimension with two pieces of evidence and the change that would raise a sub-4 score to 4; never averaged across areas.

note
three of the four F38-era deaths were the host or the session, not the model; each was recovered by a warm start and each exposed a harness defect fixed the same day (foreground arm, cutoff truncation 199 vs 200, post-cutoff resume). The one still open: a scheduled task in the user's session dies with a sign-out or shutdown
reports
14 reports in 23 days (3-25 Sep) plus this fifteenth on 29 Sep; four on 7 Sep; since the once-per-reading rule (11 Sep) the gaps are 2.2, 0.6, 1.6, 2.5, 7.0 and 3.4 days

Narrative

10 Aug: the data package, network and demand (P1-P3) merge on day 0; P4 opens the next morning when none of the 30 run-input sets loads. 11-21 Aug: the registry, the boundary-derived rebuild and every mode made physical; the first 1000-iteration arms complete; direct commits are banned on 21 Aug. 21 Aug-1 Sep: 23 families in 12 days, each arm stopped at a gate; bus is the first mode inside at a gate (1 Sep, F22). 3-11 Sep: the report series starts (nine reports in nine days); instruments, checks and the objective are rebuilt; the first result since F4 (9 Sep) reads 0 of 12. 12-14 Sep: the footpath rebuild and the engines' routing; F35's arm 0 reads 2 of 12 - the high-water mark, still unbeaten. 15-17 Sep: the two recommended controls (routers, scoring) each cost ~26 h and move nothing. 18-22 Sep: Mumbai lands as the second city (8 PRs) while Newcastle waits; the roots rebuild opens F36. 23-24 Sep: F36's arm dies at 237 of 250 to Windows Update. 25 Sep: report #14 finds three defects under demand and router; #256 fixes them and opens F37. 26-27 Sep: F37 reads 2 of 12 as a result; its tracing opens F38 (escort release, own-speed destinations, chosen motorbike). 27-29 Sep: F38's arm dies three times (session, NVMe crash, shutdown) and completes by warm starts; it reads 1 of 12, motorbike +466 %. 29 Sep: 50 days, 38 families, 6 results, goal count 2 -> 1; P4 open, P5-P7 not started.

architecture
Unchanged chain of standalone argparse scripts reading registry.load() and city.path(): build_population (B1) -> build_activity_chains (B2 tours, four binder passes, and since 9.214 a two-segment car/car-less destination kernel and the one-way escort release) -> build_matsim_plans (population_v6 per day type; since 9.214 a seeded per-person rider licence x per-household motorcycle draw written as motorbikeAvail, the carve retired under B.motorbike.representation = choice) -> build_matsim_run_inputs (per scenario x day; now re-measures the raptor access ceiling and refuses a value below it, and emits the motorbike running cost as a derived runtime value). Two new Newcastle builders derive the 9.214 inputs (build_motorcycle_possession, build_vehicle_fuel_ratio) and possession.py holds the Poisson at-least-one identity once. build_manifest gained --verify-producer (re-run a builder and byte-compare its manifest rows) used to prove the 4d4d8144 phase splits byte-identical. The 16 September SimpleNamespace staging is still the calling convention of the three largest builders (35 _types.SimpleNamespace( sites: 16 chains, 10 plans, 4 run inputs), though every mechanically generated 'One iteration of the loop' docstring is now replaced by a real contract (0 left).

severityfilelinesummaryfailure_scenarioprevious_report_status
risksrc/build/build_matsim_plans.py597motorbike_rate_lookups() falls back SILENTLY to the pooled registry vectors when either observed table is absent (if os.path.exists(rider_table) at 597, if os.path.exists(share_table) at 610): under B.motorbike.representation = choice a missing rider-licence or possession table gives every person the pooled rate, and the only trace is rider_rate_cells / household_share_sa1 = 0 in _plans_report.json.A second city (or a clean checkout that has not run build_licence_rates / build_motorcycle_possession) builds plans under choice; 35,416-person-scale availability is drawn from one pooled vector with no per-LGA or per-postcode variation and nothing refuses. Both tables are listed in OUTPUT_INPUTS, so the manifest would show the lineage the build did not use.new
risksrc/build/build_activity_chains.py3,242The 9.214 mobility kernel and the one-way escort release test car availability only (person['cav'] at 3242, lic and cav in release_oneway_drivers at 3143), because motorbike availability is drawn later, in build_matsim_plans; a car-less person with a rider licence and a household motorcycle draws destinations from the car-less (shorter) kernel and is treated as unable to drive.Part of the 35,416 motorbike-available persons (the car-less share is not reported) get destinations ~ratio x closer than their chosen vehicle supports, so their motorbike trips are short and motorbike's modelled trip length reads low against G62 for a structural reason, not a choice one. No report counts the car-less-with-motorbike cell.new
smellsrc/build/build_activity_chains.py3,162release_oneway_drivers relabels the driver's serve tour by matching the member's anchor COORDINATES ((leg['dest_x'], leg['dest_y']) == at, 3168) rather than by a driver tour id the binding row does not carry, and skips silently when the member anchor or the driver is not found (3162); no counter compares relabelled tours with released bindings.Measured on the committed WEEKDAY chains (this pass, read-only): 50,011 drop bindings released, 0 'escorted' serve tours left without a binding, 0 released tours at a destination a kept binding of the same driver uses - the risk did not materialise on this build. But 2,671 of 70,461 kept bindings have no escorted serve tour of their driver at the member's anchor coordinates, so coordinate matching is not an identity; a future placement change can leave an orphan serve tour held at car with nobody to carry, and no count would say so.new
risksrc/build/build_matsim_run_inputs.py2,469STILL OPEN: the framework assembler's main() loads params/C1_parameters.json unconditionally (2515) and assemble_scenario calls scoring_from_c1 unconditionally (2469), ignoring the bound_fields gate c1_scoring() honours (1370); the scenario list is still read from the Newcastle-shaped E1 table (2518).A bound_fields city (Mumbai) cannot use the framework assembler and keeps its own 317-line cities/mumbai/build/build_baseline_run_inputs.py; a fix to assembly (the access-ceiling refusal added this session, 2498-2500) reaches Newcastle only.still open
smellsrc/build/build_matsim_plans.py1,219The leaf-mix repair loop gives up silently after four passes (for _ in range(4)); with fix = MOTORBIKE for a car-less rider a leaf that still mixes is written as-is, and nothing counts plans left mixed.Measured clean on this build (leaf_mix_repairs tours 0 on WEEKDAY), so latent: a plan left mixed is the state ChooseRandomLegModeForSubtour refuses; GatedSubtourModeChoice stands aside for it (a logged demand defect) rather than the builder refusing it.new
smellsrc/build/build_matsim_plans.py641B1_synthetic_population.csv is read three times and B1_households.csv three times in one plans build (434, 641, 1848 / 445, 644, 1595), and twice more in build_activity_chains (3495, 3581) - 'the same CSV parsed twice'.No correctness effect; each read is 1-3 s with usecols (reasoned, not timed), and each is a separate place a column rename must be followed.new

simplification
  • build_matsim_plans.py (2,138 lines): writes population_v6 per day type from B2 chains and B1 attributes. Simplest correct form: a per-person pure function (rows, attrs) -> (attributes, plans) plus one writer loop; ~1,200-1,400 lines. Gap: the SimpleNamespace staging and module-level mutable dicts (_MOTORBIKE_Q, _MOTO_AVAIL) add ~300 lines of plumbing. Ledger row filed.
  • build_activity_chains.py (3,781 lines, +234 since the fourteenth report): tours, destinations and four binder passes. The binders are genuinely distinct; the gap is the namespace plumbing and calibrate_decay's closures (185 lines). Medium gap; ledger row filed (dataclasses).
  • build_matsim_run_inputs.py (2,609 lines): the Newcastle-shaped main() duplicated by Mumbai's 317-line assembler - one layer with one real consumer per city. Ledger row filed.
  • possession.py (55 lines): the identity once; no gap.
  • build_motorcycle_possession.py (209 lines): geometry join + identity + assertion; a thin script, gap small.
  • build_manifest.py verify_producer (170 lines): re-implements a before/after byte comparison the manifest already records; the simplest form re-runs the builder into a temp city root instead of overwriting the package and restoring it - see the contract-and-checks finding on crash safety.
testability
Improved and now on the result path: test_plans_writer_tiers (13 tests) imports build_matsim_plans and writes each tier - the check that would have caught the fourteenth report's external-tier drop - and write_day now refuses any tier whose written count differs from the chains (build_matsim_plans.py:1772-1776). test_motorbike_choice (35 tests, 396 lines) covers the rider/possession draws, riders_first clipping, the leaf repair and the runtime cost entry; test_escort_release_and_mobility_kernel covers release and the two-segment solve. Still untested: build_population (imported by no unit test - it is in uncovered_src_modules), build_manifest.verify_producer's crash behaviour, build_params, build_network_layers, attach_gradient/speed_zones and 13 modules code_metrics lists as dead (nothing under src/ imports them; several are imported only by city scripts and have their own tests, e.g. ordered_route_geometry).

architecture
run.py resolves the overlay and overrides, runs the issue gate once, preflights every refusal (refuse_unsafe_host now reads a pending restart and the Windows Update pause against the run's ceiling), then by default re-invokes itself under the Task Scheduler (_detach writes a .cmd wrapper that adds --issue-gate-passed --foreground --scheduled-child). The scheduled child runs run_matsim.run(): subsample, emit config, start the JVM with three daemon watchers (ceiling, stall, gate) and the progress monitor, and close out through close_out() with a completion from the stop markers. New since the fourteenth report: died (stop_run records a run whose recorded pids are all dead as died, run_matsim.py:2149,2173), warm start from a died record or from a stopped_by_operator record with --stopped-was-death TEXT (resolve_warm_start 177-260), an innovation fraction rounded UP and 0.0 past the cutoff (warm_start_overrides 1068-1082), a resume overlay that already carries first_iteration = N passed through untouched (1060-1067), and refuse_foreground_arm (run.py:377-399). run() itself was split into phases (8b21655d); the four stop-cause readers now share _marker (1915-1925).
findings
severityfilelinesummaryfailure_scenarioprevious_report_status
riskrun.py388The foreground refusal is bypassed by the flag it exempts: refuse_foreground_arm returns when a.scheduled_child is set, and --scheduled-child is an ordinary (help-suppressed) argparse flag that _detach itself appends to the child's argv (504) - so copying the scheduled child's command line, exactly how F38's arm 0 was launched to its death, still launches an arm in the foreground. On POSIX the check never fires at all (a launch without --foreground is in the foreground there, 363-368).A session reads results/_launch/citysim_run_<stamp>.cmd (or the child's argv in a process list), re-runs it from a background shell to 'resume quickly', and the arm dies with the session again - 9.215's death at iteration 79 (13 h) repeated; the refusal prints nothing because the copied flags include --scheduled-child.new
defectsrc/run/run_matsim.py1,060warm_start_overrides trusts a resume overlay: when the overlay already sets first_iteration = N it returns without comparing the overlay's cutoff with the parent's, and prints the cutoff with %.0f (1066), which rounds 199.99998 up to '200' while the jar truncates it to 199 - the exact failure 9.215 found (resumed at 175, f = 0.333333). tests/unit/test_warm_start_after_death.py:135 asserts that nothing is re-derived and nothing is checked.The next hand-written resume overlay (three were written this session: f38_baseline_resume175_25pct with 0.333334, resume225 with 0.0) carries 0.333333; the launch log says 'innovation cutoff at iteration 200', innovation stops at 199, and the resumed arm is a different search from its parent with nothing in _run.json to show it.new
risksrc/run/procs.py41pid_alive treats any OpenProcess failure as 'dead' (if not handle: return False), including ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED; since 9.215 stop_run turns 'every recorded pid dead' into completion died (run_matsim.py:2149,2173), and died is the one completion resolve_warm_start accepts without a reason (225-226). Liveness is the only evidence: the run's own log is not read to confirm the JVM ended without an exception, and pid reuse within one boot (the fourteenth report's procs.py:72 risk) is still undetected.(a) A task started elevated or under another account is unreadable to a non-elevated shell: --stop kills nothing (the loop at 2153-2161 skips 'dead' pids), records died, and the JVM keeps running while the record says it died; the concurrent-arm refusal (tasklist) is then the only thing between it and a warm-started twin. (b) A JVM that died of an exception after the harness was killed is recorded died and is warm-startable, carrying the defect forward.new (extends the fourteenth report's procs.py:72 pid-reuse risk, still open)
riskrun.py532The detached task is created without /RU or /IT options, i.e. 'run only when the user is logged on' in the user's session; a sign-out or Start-menu shutdown ends it. refuse_unsafe_host guards Windows Update only.Recorded on the f38_baseline_resume225_25pct overlay: the warm-started F38 arm died at iteration 229 'when the host was shut down from the Start menu (a shutdown or logoff ends the user-session scheduled task)' - the third death of one arm in two days; nothing at launch warns that a logoff kills it.new
smellcities/newcastle/overlays/runs/f38_baseline_resume175_25pct.json1Each resume is a hand-written copy of the base overlay (29-33 lines, 90 % identical) with a hand-derived fraction under allow_outside_sweep; the copied justification text still says 'This arm declares RUN.gate.wall_ceiling_h = 34' while the overlays set 18 and 4.The record a reader cites for the resumed arm states a ceiling it did not run under; and the fraction the harness can derive (warm_start_overrides) is typed by a person because the set layer refuses the derived value outside the sweep.new
smellsrc/run/run_matsim.py1,608PARTLY FIXED: the four stop-cause readers now share _marker (1915-1925), but the three watcher loops (ceiling 1608-1667, stall 1670-1728, gate 1731-1874) still repeat the poll/marker/kill skeleton; start_gate_watch is the area's longest function (144 lines, complexity 34).A change to the kill path (e.g. recording the pid killed, or a grace period) lands in one watcher and not the others.still open (partly fixed)

simplification
  • run.py (556 lines): a CLI that resolves, preflights and detaches. Simplest form ~300 lines; the gap is the stop/close-out/list branches and the detach wrapper, each justified. Small gap.
  • run_matsim.py (2,786 lines): launch, JVM, three watchers, close-out, stop, orphan close-out, warm start. The watchers are the one large simplification (one skeleton); the rest is distinct behaviour. Ledger row filed.
  • procs.py (187 lines): liveness and host state once; no structural gap.
  • resume overlays: a bespoke per-resume file where the harness already derives every value; ledger row filed.
  • src/setup/extract_loop_body.py and split_stages.py (564 lines, complexity 39 and 32): refactoring tools that generated the SimpleNamespace staging; with the docstrings now hand-written they have no remaining consumer beyond test_refactor_tools - a candidate for deletion once the dataclass change lands.
testability
Good and growing: test_warm_start_after_death (13 tests: died, --stopped-was-death, the rounded-up fraction, the post-cutoff 0.0, the foreground refusal), test_stop_run, test_close_out/_orphan, test_prune_and_reemit (17 functions), test_refuse_unsafe_host, test_trim_grace (now with the failing-extractor case), test_ceiling_watch, test_heap_rule_and_stall_watch. Gaps: the tests enshrine two of the risks above (test_a_resume_overlay_is_not_re_derived checks that nothing is re-derived and nothing validated; test_a_probe_and_the_schedulers_child_may_run_in_the_foreground passes on the flag alone), pid_alive's Windows branch has no test, and session_gate/issue_gate.measurement_due are uncovered.

architecture
Readers over a run directory: extract_metrics (the _metrics.json snapshot; since aee68825 also the road-vehicle speed tail and stuck agents, folded into the one trips-table pass it already made), report_mode_ridership (the twelve-mode reader the board, the gate watcher and the objective share; since this session the station-entries mode also reads rail legs, trip shapes and station pairs), transit_link_delays (new --road from telemetry_links.json and --access from one iteration's trips table, over one parse of the network), measure_bound_trips (held-tour spill), iteration_trips, report_choice_set_coverage (now run-aware of the motorbike representation), fit.py and calibrate.py (the objective and the search). run_value() reads a registry value as the run resolved it from its own _config.json (extract_metrics.py:213-221).

simplification
  • report_mode_ridership.py (1,493 lines): the twelve-mode reader. Simplest correct form: rows function + printers, ~900-1,000 lines. Gap ~500 lines; ledger row filed.
  • run_view.py (1,485 lines): an HTTP viewer with its own log scan; distinct behaviour, but scan/make_handler (nesting 11) would halve with a small router table. Medium gap (not read).
  • transit_link_delays.py (372 lines): two readers over one network parse - already the simple form.
  • extract_metrics.RoadSpeed: folded into the existing trips pass rather than a second read - the simple form.
testability
Strong additions: test_iteration_trips_derivation (182 lines; the fourteenth report's to-4 item for iteration_trips.derive and mode_share_at), test_station_entries, test_road_speed, test_road_link_delays, test_held_tour_spill, test_coverage_run_aware, test_person_attributes, test_calibrate_search_space's all-infeasible case, and test_fit additions. Uncovered: run_view (1,485 lines), baseline_behaviour (cx 56), measure_corridor_composition/measure_demographic_modes, measure_reading_stability, profile_run, diagnose_* (code_metrics uncovered list).

architecture
The registry (cities/<city>/registry/*.json against config/schema/) is resolved by src/registry/__init__.py and guarded by check_hardcoding (the literal ledger, at 0), check_city, check_legacy_drift and render_schema/render_docs; documents by check_doc_currency (now with --fix), check_doc_shape and check_doc_links; data by check_manifest (now with a 'recorded debt' of paths allowed to fail two provenance rules, which may only shrink); secrets by check_secrets (new, also a pre-commit hook). session_gate.py runs them in one pass; CI (.github/workflows/test.yml) runs the committed-file subset. New fields this session: B.motorbike.representation/rider_coupling/possession_identity, B.population.rider_licence_rate_by_age_band/household_motorcycle_share, B.activity.escort_oneway_scope/destination_mobility, C.scoring.motorbike_fuel_ratio; RUN.transit_router.access_max_radius_m re-measured (55,900 m) with an assembler refusal below its re-measured reach.

smelltests/check_doc_currency.py458--fix rewrites every DRIFTED live-state number to its artefact's value in place, which turns the currency check from a refusal into a formatter: a figure the prose argues from (e.g. 'walk 6x observed') is updated without the sentence that interprets it.A handoff runs --fix, the gate goes green, and a position page states a new number beside an old conclusion; the check's purpose - making a person look at the sentence the number moved under - is skipped. (Implementation matches the check's own finditer semantics; the risk is the workflow.)new

simplification
  • check_manifest.py (861 lines): a hash/size/licence/lineage check is ~250 lines; the other ~600 are city data. Ledger row filed.
  • check_hardcoding.py (1,584 lines): its STRUCTURAL_INLINE allowlist is keyed by function name, so every phase split churns the allowlist (22 keys renamed in this session's diff); keying by a content hash of the statement would survive refactors. Medium gap.
  • check_doc_currency.py (680 lines): the --fix half (~110 lines) makes the check self-healing; the simpler and stricter form is report-only. Finding filed.
  • check_secrets.py (81 lines): already minimal.
testability
Checks are now themselves tested: test_check_manifest_dates (9), test_check_secrets, test_check_city_fields (18), test_doc_currency_fix (5), test_verify_producer (7), test_import_sweep. Gaps: nothing tests that RECORDED_DEBT cannot grow; check_city_agnostic has no case for city data inside tests/; render_docs, session_gate and sweep_ledger are imported by no unit test; the Java probes (run_signal_probes.py) are not run in CI because the toolchain is not in the CI image.

architecture
Unchanged since the fourteenth report: per-city extract scripts write data/raw/ with provenance_*.json and processed tables; build_manifest.py composes MANIFEST.csv/.json from OUTPUT_INPUTS declarations and provenance records. This session added one Newcastle acquisition (ABS SMVU 9208.0 data cube, provenance_smvu.json) and the processed motorcycle-possession and rider-licence tables (produced by city build scripts, reviewed under the build layer). The extract directories changed only by the removal of the timing-footer import (5 one-line edits).

architecture
CitysimControler.assemble() (607 lines at the fourteenth report) is now a 45-line sequence: a Groups holder materialises the 22 config groups, then routingBindings, installChargeHandlers, installBeforeMobsimEngines, installQSim, installScoring, installPtExtensions, installIncomeScoring and telemetry install in order (CitysimControler.java:147-291). Before the Controler is built, AvailabilityModesCalculator.applyMotorbikeChoiceSet adds motorbike to subtourModeChoice.modes and chainBasedModes when the population carries motorbikeAvail (201-217) - a derived transformation, logged, so the emitted config is identical under carve and choice. EscortCoherenceListener.notifyReplanning (380 lines) is split into a Pass holder and proposePassengers/proposeDrivers phases; GatedSubtourModeChoice's 236-line anonymous run is gate() plus named phases; HouseholdVehicleRoster, SubtourChainScan and ActivityLinkAssigner likewise. ActivityRetimes (89 lines) is now the one retime/restore used by the ride engine and by the taxi fleet, which finally executes a served passenger's wait (TaxiFleetEngine.java:263-265) and restores it after the mobsim (390-395). PtFareChargeHandler refuses a boarded submode with no declared fare (180-184, 667-690). No Java method is now over 150 lines (code_metrics long_methods 7 -> 0).

Verdict on the 250-iteration horizon

The 250-iteration horizon is reachable at the measured pace, and was reached twice this period: F37's arm 0 in 31.4 h uninterrupted and F38's arm 0 in 30.89 h of kept iterations across four joined runs (37.0 h elapsed). On an idle host the plain pace is ~346 s innovating and 270-315 s after the cutoff, so a clean 25 % arm on the F38 build costs ~27 h (F37's would have been ~25.9 h without its 5.56 h co-tenant stretch); arm_cost's 32.0 h quote was about right for a host that is not idle. What cost hours this period was the host - 5.56 h and 4.07 h of shared CPU and paging on the last two arms, 2.86 h discarded to three deaths plus 2.4 h of idle calendar time waiting for a person to resume, and F36's 24.2 h lost before the update-pause guard existed. Halving the arm (~13.5 h) is not reachable by the result-preserving levers: host quiet (4-5.6 h when a co-tenant would run), replanning back toward its pre-footpath 25 s (0-2.7 h, unmeasured), input routing at build (0.45 h), GC or heap (unmeasured) sum to at most ~3-4 h on an idle host. The mobsim is now 67 % of the arm (20.7 h) and grew 10-16 % with F38's demand; only a mobsim change - most plausibly walk off the qsim, which reverses F34 and opens a family - or a shorter innovating horizon, both of which touch results, could halve it, and the mobsim's split is unmeasured - needs a probe arm.

The project in its field

_total

what the comparison says

  • The top rung is still ours alone: no row of 58 scores every simulated mode against a banded target; the reading on it fell from 2 to 1 of 12 inside 10 % (car) at F38's arm 0, with nine modes past the 20 % stop bar.
  • Mode-share fits in the field are routinely tight where the model calibrates few modes against one survey (Berlin v6: MAE < 0.5 pp over five modes; Rotterdam-The Hague and Jakarta on shares and distances) - the difficulty here is twelve simultaneous targets on three bases, not any one of them.
  • Transit-by-operator validation in the agency world spans the same range this project sees: PSRC SoundCast +7 % (Metro, peak) to +50 % (Kitsap) and +45 % off-peak (Sound Transit); PSRC's ferry -82 % and monorail -98 % in 2010 - small fixed-route modes miss by large factors elsewhere too.
  • The MATSim community's current work (MUM 2026: seven calibration and optimisation talks, a distributed mobsim) is moving toward automated multi-source and online calibration - where this project's ASC contraction test and single-artefact _fit.json already sit - but none yet publishes a per-mode band.
  • Physical fidelity is still unrewarded in print: the only stored case where finer physics moved a validation figure is Berlin v6's SUMO-calibrated free speeds; Barcelona runs actuated signals at 2,035 intersections for R2 0.81 on counts only.
  • Agencies in this project's own country are adopting ActivitySim (TfNSW for Greater Sydney, validation due by late 2027; Victoria's DTP shadow-running VITM-2) - neither covers Newcastle and neither has published a fit, so this project remains the only validated agent-based model of the Newcastle region in the library.
  • Single-line smartcard 'validations' (AAAM Beijing, Riga) validate demand against itself; the tap-data rung is meaningful only where demand is synthesised independently of the taps (Cape Town, Seoul, Munich, and this project's Opal targets).
  • No row changed a fit in the four days since the fourteenth pass; every change in the comparison this pass is on this repository's side.

what each does best that we lack

fromitem
MATSim Open Berlin v6 (Rakow & Nagel ASC tool)An automated closed-form ASC loop actually RUN to convergence (about five runs, mode-share MAE < 0.5 pp) - this project's asc_fixed_point.py is built and held, never run.
Okanagan ASIM + MATSim (Saha & Fatmi 2026)A constrained tour-level bike destination-choice sub-module ahead of MATSim - the structural lever for bike at +143.6 % and 7.36 km against the observed length.

does_best
Every controllable value declared with units, provenance and a sweep or held-fixed rule (558 fields), every living number pinned to its artefact by a check, twelve modes each monitored on its own basis every tenth iteration - and, unique in this survey, a REGISTRY-ENFORCED refusal to fit a constant over a named missing mechanism (C.asc.rail's departure_requires clause). THIS PASS ADDS A SECOND UNIQUENESS, AND IT IS THE HONEST ONE: the project's _fit.json scores mode shares, absolute link counts, trip-length geometry and vehicle occupancy IN ONE ARTEFACT FROM ONE RUN, names its 32 unscorable targets with reasons, and refuses to call a stopped arm a result. Across 53 surveyed projects, the closest published analogues each hold part of that - AToM validates five observation families but calibrates against one; Tallinn optimises three target families at once but reports no per-mode transit rung; the WFRC framework enumerates every validation summary but states outright that not all of them can be met simultaneously. | 2026-09-14: against the three rows added this pass the position sharpens - Cape Town reaches the tap-data rung for ONE operator with ~25 hand-tuned runs; mobiTopp Stuttgart calibrates trip-length distributions and modal split jointly but by eye; Barcelona runs generic actuated signals at 2,035 intersections on full demand for R2 0.81 on counts and nothing else. None scores more than one observation family with a stated band. | 2026-09-14 (tenth pass): the claim is now made against a RESULT with 2 of 12 inside 10 % rather than 0 - and against 55 rows. The two rows added this pass sharpen it from opposite sides: Barcelona-MATSim (2019) validates on counts it never calibrated on and names the excluded demand behind its two misses, which this project's fit module does for its 32 unscorable targets but not yet for its count outliers; Rotterdam-The Hague (2025) removes mode choice from MATSim altogether so that one calibrator owns it, where this project's twelve constants are scored inside the co-evolutionary loop they must survive. | 2026-09-16 (twelfth pass): the claim is now made against a THIRD result and the first one-field pair reading in the survey - a null. The BEAM update this pass sharpens the comparison from the sample side: BEAM calibrates at 10 %, validates at 30 % and states that an on-demand mode needs 40 % before its dynamics settle; this project reads taxi and ride at 25 % and scores them with a band, which no BEAM paper does. | 2026-09-29 (fifteenth pass): unchanged against 58 rows. The MUM 2026 programme shows the MATSim community moving toward automated multi-source calibration (seven talks), where this project's contraction test and _fit.json already sit; none of those talks yet publishes a per-mode band.

the fifteenth report, 29 September 2026
calibration_method
family
coordinate descent over registry-declared sweeps (unchanged, never run to completion) PLUS, NEW AT THIS HEAD AND NEVER RUN, an implemented ASC log-ratio fixed point - src/calibrate/asc_fixed_point.py. Read from the source at HEAD 781cf41, not searched.
what_the_source_says
src/calibrate/calibrate.py:508-533 runs coordinate descent: per round, per free parameter, per grid point across that parameter's registry sweep, a FULL MATSim run -> extract_metrics -> fit -> a weighted scalar objective over declared _fit.json components, keeping a point only if it is feasible against the C4 occupancy and trip-length constraints; the round stops when the improvement falls below delta. The search space is DERIVED FROM THE REGISTRY, not listed in the script: a field is free only if it is source=assumed, carries a scalar sweep and is not held_fixed. The six mode constants are held_fixed under DECISIONS.md 8.5 and are therefore unreachable from the loop by construction - so this project has NO log(target/modelled) ASC loop of any kind. The one constant that is solved, asc_car_passenger, is solved in a separate module (solve_asc_ride.py:101-121) by SECANT INTERPOLATION ON log(ride_per_car) against the observed passenger:driver ratio, and is reported as a CONSTRAINT, not an estimate. The loop refuses to fit more parameters than the objective identifies (four independent numbers) and cannot read a holdout row by construction.
objective_evaluations
coordinate descent: calibrate.py:433 computes the budget as sum(len(grid(p, points_per_param)) for p in free) * max_rounds FULL MATSim runs; at 25% an arm reaching iteration 100 cost 7.66 h wall, so a 3-point 3-parameter 2-round search is ~18 arms and ~140 machine hours. THE LOOP HAS NEVER BEEN RUN TO COMPLETION. The ASC fixed point is far cheaper by design - TWO arms settle the contraction question - and is the only one of the two that has an evaluation count small enough to state in advance. Neither has been executed.
convergence_criterion
round-over-round objective improvement < delta

2026-09-11
verified_in
20260911_project_report (eighth pass, at 20ae4e9)
asc_fixed_point_new_this_pass
src/calibrate/asc_fixed_point.py EXISTS and PROPOSES ONLY; it has NEVER BEEN RUN. It applies delta_i = damping * [ ln(target_i/modelled_i) - ln(target_ref/modelled_ref) ] against car as reference - the same object as the Berry (1994) share inversion and the same expression matsim-vsp/matsim-python-tools computes verbatim, with the damping declared on CAL.asc.damping. THREE DESIGN CHOICES ARE UNUSUAL AND WORTH RECORDING: (1) THE TEST IS THE CONTRACTION, NOT THE SHARES - two damped rounds are compared mode by mode, and if |delta| shrinks the residual is a taste that constants will close, while if it does not shrink the deficit is STRUCTURAL and no constant will ever close it. That is a cheap, decisive, published-in-spirit answer to the 'constant or mechanism' question that the agency protocol answers only by ordering. (2) It CANNOT REACH A FROZEN CONSTANT: it reads held_fixed from the registry and refuses, structurally, the way the holdout guard refuses. (3) On heavy rail and light rail the step is marked basis_is_boardings and reported as a PROXY, because the logit inversion is exact only for shares of a common total. ROUND 1 IS PROPOSED OFF THE F31 GATE AND NOT APPLIED: bike -0.5121, bus -0.2301, ferry +0.6713, light rail +0.4143.
read_against_the_field_this_pass
Two published methods now bracket this project's loop and both were read this pass. (1) Chen, Prakash, Lima de Azevedo & Ben-Akiva (2020, TR-C 119:102650) names this project's coordinate descent for what it is: 'myopic heuristics in the sense that they do not consider the fundamental inter-dependencies among choice-models and do not have a systematic way to adjust model parameters' - and their non-myopic alternative calibrates precisely this project's target set at once (activity shares, mode shares, mode-specific OD flows, mode-specific sensor counts). (2) The WFRC/RSG ActivitySim framework publishes New ASC = Old ASC + ln(Target Proportion / Model Proportion) verbatim - the exact expression in asc_fixed_point.py - inside a CYCLIC protocol over model components, and states plainly that 'It is typically the case that not all calibration and validation targets can be achieved simultaneously.' | 2026-09-11 (third bracket): POLARIS's published procedure is the same cyclic ASC-by-purpose update, TIERED by iteration so that 'network state' can 'adapt to this change in demand' before the next tier is touched, with counts and speeds explicitly outside the calibrated set ('can only be minimally calibrated'). MATSim-NYC calibrated NO demand constants at all - its nested logit was estimated on RHTS + TLC/FHV/Citi Bike records and converted analytically - and spent its six SPSA iterations on twelve link-CAPACITY factors instead, which is the opposite allocation to this project's.

warm_start
--warm-start <dead run dir> exists in src/run/run_matsim.py: it resumes from a dead run's newest plans checkpoint, records warm_started_from, and RE-DERIVES RUN.replanning.fraction_to_disable_innovation so the innovation cutoff ITERATION is the parent's (the pinned jar's first + f x (last - first) had moved it 240 -> 260 at N = 100, #192). It is crash recovery, not a bit-identical continuation. Arm 0 of F35 dumps plans every 25 iterations for exactly this reason (the F33 arm that died at 98 had checkpoints at it.0 and it.1 only). This matches the platform's own answer (MATSim issue #834: restart AT the checkpoint iteration from its post-replanning plans); the platform has no finer mechanism, and the interval is the whole trade. No surveyed row states a warm start from a prior calibrated run except as the matsim-scenarios practice of shipping calibrated output plans as input plans. | 2026-09-14: arm 0 ran with warm_started_from: null and dumped plans every 25 iterations (12 checkpoints, 522-898 s each, ~50 min of the arm); it did not need the mechanism. Across the field this pass, Kelheim v3.0's published output is a single 388-iteration run and Rotterdam-The Hague's outer loop restarts MATSim per LOS iteration; no surveyed row checkpoints or warm-starts a calibration arm.

MATSim (Multi-Agent Transport Simulation)Java; co-evolutionary iterative agent-based simulation - demand, mobsim (queue-based traffic flow), replanning, iteration control, analysis, all modular and replaceableGNU General Public License - declared in the <licenses> block of matsim/pom.xml on master: name 'GNU General Public License', url ${basedir}/COPYING, comments 'See also the LICENSE file'. Note that GitHub's own licence API returns 404 for the repository (no SPDX-detectable LICENSE at the root), which is why four earlier fetch routes found nothing; scenario repositories built on it record GPL-2.0 for their own code. CLOSED this pass.MATSim 2026 (Spring), released 10 April 2026; annual April cadence since 2020 (2025 Spring 11 Apr 2025, 2024 Spring 13 Apr 2024)yes by design - a toolbox, not a city model; every city is a scenario built on itThe reference open engine for large-scale agent-based transport simulation: a replaceable-component architecture in which route, mode, departure time and plan memory are all co-evolved to a stochastic user equilibriumBerlin, Leipzig, Kelheim, Lausitz, Metropole Ruhr, Duesseldorf, Dresden, Hannover, Kyoto, Mexico City, Santiago de Chile, Gauteng, New York City (NYU C2SMART), Vienna (AIT), Melbourne (AToM), Tyne and Wear (Newcastle University), Dhaka, Brussels, Infrastructure Victoria MABM, Wellington NZ (in build) - all from this library's project rows

eqasim (MATSim extension + open pipelines)MATSim plus discrete-choice mode models and a synthetic-population pipeline run from raw open dataGPL-2.0v2.2.0, published 3 June 2026 (v2.1.0 19 February 2026; v2.0.0 8 May 2025) - the release date was 'unknown' in the library until this passpartly - the pipeline is designed to be re-pointed at a new city, but each city carries its own scenario repositoryTurns raw public datasets into a runnable, reproducible MATSim scenario end to end - the closest published analogue of this project's build layerIle-de-France / Paris, California (SF Bay, LA, San Diego), Sao Paulo, Switzerland / Zurich, Bavaria, Montreal (auckland-example is a demo only)

The document and process layer

controls

permissions
  • strip-session-ref.yml: pull-requests: write on pull_request (not _target), body passed through env - needs write to edit the body; safe against injection.
  • pages.yml: contents read, pages write, id-token write - the minimum for Pages deploy.
  • test.yml: contents read.
  • SessionStart hooks set the repo's git identity, core.hooksPath and commit.gpgsign=false on every session; gate-pr-on-docs.sh runs four Python checks with the user's privileges on every matching Bash call and fails closed.
  • Sandbox allowlist: ~150 domains including broad hosts (s3.amazonaws.com, github.com, raw.githubusercontent.com, archive.org) and the rolled-back Nagpur/Pune hosts (livewebapi.metrorailnagpur.com, www.metrorailnagpur.com, punemetrorail.org) - more than any live city needs.
  • .claude/settings.local.json (gitignored) enables LangSmith tracing (CC_LANGSMITH_* env): session transcripts leave the machine to a third party outside the sandbox allowlist; the key is no longer tracked (check_secrets + pre-commit) but the egress is recorded nowhere in the conventions.
pinning
by_hash
  • GitHub Actions (every uses: pinned to a commit SHA)
  • the toolchain (.tools/toolchain.json sha256)
  • pt2matsim by digest
by_name_or_version
  • requirements.txt (== versions, #206 decision-needed)
  • the Pages CDN asset by version (#206)
  • python-version "3.14"
  • runs-on ubuntu-latest
licence_boundary
Visible: MANIFEST.csv per row (732 CC-BY / 220 ODbL / 15 bespoke, STATUS.md:77), README.md:191/215, the Pages footer (build_pages.py:115) and the report footer (render_report.py:560). Not stated on run outputs (results/ is unpublished) - acceptable. The recommendation ledger still lists the footer line as open.

verdict

FOLLOWED FAST, AND FOLLOWING HAS NOT MOVED THE GOAL COUNT. Over 14 reports and 241 recommendations, 186 are taken (77.2 %), 4 overtaken, 18 repeated and 33 open. Report #14 is the most-followed report of the series: 12 of 16 taken, every model recommendation within hours (PR #256, 25 Sep), and it was right on substance - the missing external tier, the misderived 1,200 m access cap and the unexecuted taxi wait were real defects under every F36 reading. The scoreboard after following it: F37 read 2 of 12 (car +3.7 %, motorbike -7.2 %) - the same count as F35 - and F38, built on F37's tracing, read 1 of 12, with heavy rail +118 % and a chosen motorbike +466 %. Across the whole series the goal count at a result has been 0 (F32), 2 (F35, three times), 2 (F37), 1 (F38): the only step up (9-14 Sep) crossed a boundary that bundled three changes and cannot be attributed. What the reports demonstrably buy is correctness: the holdout leak (#152), the count map on the wrong roads (#176), the reader that read heavy rail 0 (#201), unscaled standing room (#240), and #14's three defects. What they do not buy is the fit, because each family ships one arm and no pair, so no recommendation's effect on a mode is ever measured against a control inside its family; and because the recurring asks that would make a deviation readable - the replication band (9th issuance), the TfNSW tables (8th) - are never taken. Report #13's rec 7 (a passing package audit before an arm) is now unfollowed across three families. The ledger drifts on five rows and one row has been flagged by three consecutive reports. CADENCE: 15 reports in 26 days; the once-per-reading rule is kept (3.4 days, one reading of two results). AGAINST THIS REPORT: any recommendation below that re-asks the replication band (10th), the TfNSW request (9th), a second reviewer (4th), build timing (5th), the package audit before an arm (2nd), travel-time validation (2nd) or ledger hygiene for 20260916T062246:11 (4th) is a repeat, and should become a lane decision taken once or be dropped; any model recommendation should state the deviation it predicts, so report #16 can score it.

finding
Two model changes reached results in four days - the fastest loop in the project - and neither raised the count: F37 held 2 of 12 and F38 fell to 1. Both did what they were built to do (the external tier returned, the access cap stopped cutting pt, walk and bike shortened, the rail excess fell) and each exposed a missing term in the choice (a motorbike with no constant; pt reaching far stations with no distance-banded router cost). Every family since F35 has had one arm and no control, so no single change's effect on a mode has been measured.
whole_history
The goal count at a result: 0 (F32, 9 Sep) -> 2 (F35 x3, 14-16 Sep) -> 2 (F37, 27 Sep) -> 1 (F38, 29 Sep). At gates: bus inside at F22, car at F28 and F31. Car and motorbike are the only modes ever inside at a result.

Recommendations, ranked

Ranked by what each would prevent or move × how cheap it is. A repeat tag marks one an earlier report already issued; the process audit counts them against this report too.

  1. Charge the service interval: switch C.time_weights.service_quality_representation from absent to headway (and put the headway into the raptor's submode trade). model opens a family
    The deviation pattern is the one a frequency penalty corrects: infrequent heavy rail +118 % and ferry +116 % are over, frequent bus -30 % and light rail -72.5 % are under; PDFH's frequency penalty (0.45-1.0 x headway by band) and Wheat & Wardman 2017 (weight 1.001) support it; the mechanism is built (ServiceQualityScoring) and has never run.
    change: C.time_weights.service_quality_representation (C_behaviour.json); the raptor half through C.raptor.mode_cost_representation (absent today) · guard: report_mode_ridership.py --stations per-station entries; the four pt submodes against their targets
  2. Make motorcycle availability a daily probability, not possession: scale household possession by the ABS SMVU use ratio (motorcycles 1.9k km a year against 11.1k for a passenger vehicle). model opens a family
    Motorbike is +465.7 % chosen at a zero constant; SMVU (already in the package as the fuel-ratio source) says a household motorcycle is used on a small fraction of days - the mechanism, not a fitted constant.
    change: B.motorbike.* (a derived daily-availability field from SMVU Table 1 km per vehicle), build_matsim_plans.draw_motorbike_availability; relabel C.asc.motorbike from definition to assumed with a sweep · guard: motorbike share and trip length at the next result; a unit test on the daily draw
  3. Measure one change at a time: a control pair inside the family before the next family opens, and the replication band from three seeds. process repeat ×10
    186/241 recommendations taken, goal count 2, 2, 1: F38 changed four things and regressed ride and bus with no attribution; the replication band is on its 10th issuance.
    change: a lane decision asked once (lane.json): pair design for F39; CAL.objective.replication_band_pp · guard: compare_runs.py --modes inside a family
  4. Close the foreground bypass: the scheduler wrapper sets a launch stamp the harness checks against a live scheduled task; drop --scheduled-child from the copyable argv. code
    run.py:388 exempts --scheduled-child and run.py:504 writes it into the child command line, so copying it (how F38 died at 79) still launches an arm that dies with its shell.
    change: run.py refuse_foreground_arm and _detach · guard: test_warm_start_after_death: a copied child argv is refused
  5. Check a resume overlay's cutoff as the jar computes it (int(first + f x (last-first))) and refuse a mismatch with the parent's. code
    run_matsim.py:1060 trusts the overlay and prints %.0f, which shows 200 where the jar truncates to 199.
    change: src/run/run_matsim.py warm_start_overrides · guard: a unit test with f = 0.333333 refused
  6. Make an arm survive sign-out and a reboot: register the task to run whether or not the user is signed in, resume a died arm on boot from its newest checkpoint within its approval, and record host load in the progress digest. code
    F38 lost 7.24 machine-hours and 2.37 h idle to three deaths and a co-tenant; only 1 of the last 3 arms ran clean.
    change: run.py _detach (schtasks /RU with S4U), a boot task calling --warm-start, _progress.json host CPU/RAM · guard: a detached smoke survives a sign-out
  7. Rewrite the board's hand lines and the five stale position pages from F38's result, pin the run state and "N of 12" in doc_currency.json, and open the session PR. process
    STATUS.md:10-11/:23/:74 contradict the generated block; 21 contradictions with every document gate green; the branch is 19 commits and 4 days ahead of main, so /onboard reads F37 as running.
    change: docs/STATUS.md, docs/positions/*, cities/newcastle/tests/doc_currency.json · guard: check_doc_currency.py --strict with the new claims
  8. Bound the pt no-route fallback: a pt request with no transit route is not answered with a walk longer than the walk mode's observed reach. model opens a family
    NetworkDirectWalkPtRouter.java:132 returns a network walk of any length; walk trips average 3.61 km against 0.70 and 39.5 % of pt requests are unconnected rural ends.
    change: src/java/citysim/NetworkDirectWalkPtRouter.java; a derived reach from C.constraint.trip_length_km.walk · guard: walk mean trip length at the next result
  9. Attach trip ends to links that can carry them (capacity-aware ActivityLinkAssigner). model opens a family
    29 service/living-street links carried trip ends needing up to 38 h of their own capacity on F37; car trips touching them averaged 72.6 min at 13.4 km/h and the car mean is 25.0 min against the HTS 17.2.
    change: src/java/citysim/ActivityLinkAssigner.java (lane task access-link-capacity) · guard: transit_link_delays.py --access --top 50
  10. Run check_manifest for every city in the gate and CI, and turn the recorded debts into per-rule ceilings that may only fall. code
    Mumbai reports 11 sha256 failures while every gate is green; the provenance debt grew this session (check_manifest.py:852).
    change: src/run/session_gate.py, .github/workflows/test.yml, tests/check_manifest.py · guard: the gate itself
  11. Read a died run's liveness from its log, not an OpenProcess failure: treat access denied as unknown and stop wall_s at the log's last line. code
    procs.py:41 reads access denied as dead, and died is warm-startable without a reason; aborted_20260928T163345 carries 1.8 h of wall time after its death.
    change: src/run/procs.py, run_matsim.stop_run · guard: a unit test with an access-denied handle
  12. Price a warm start from its checkpoint and have the performance collector read _build_timing.json. code repeat ×5
    arm_cost quoted 32.4 h for a 175-iteration resume; collect_performance.py:371 hard-codes "none recorded" while _build_timing.json holds 5,648.8 s.
    change: src/analyse/arm_cost.py, .claude/skills/project-report/scripts/collect_performance.py · guard: arm_cost on a warm overlay quotes the remaining iterations
  13. Fix collect_metrics' direct-to-main count to walk origin/main. code
    It reports 24/23 direct commits that are this branch's and a squash merge; the true count after the ban is 0.
    change: .claude/skills/project-report/scripts/collect_metrics.py · guard: a fixture repo test
  14. Put the household motorcycle under the census roster in Java so two riders cannot use one drawn motorcycle at once. code opens a family
    AvailabilityModesCalculator.java:201 makes motorbike available per person; the roster maps cars only.
    change: src/java/citysim/HouseholdVehicleRoster.java · guard: a roster probe
  15. Refuse a choice build when either observed motorbike table is missing instead of falling back to the pooled vector. code
    build_matsim_plans.py:597/610 degrade silently.
    change: src/build/build_matsim_plans.py · guard: test_motorbike_choice: missing table refuses
  16. Close #257 on §9.217 and file the motorbike availability/constant as its own issue; post the six due measurements; re-aim the F35-anchored pairs at F38. process
    issue_gate lists 6 measurements due on F38; #257 is overtaken; six pair issues still name the closed F35 control.
    change: GitHub issues; issue_gate flag for an AWAITING-RUN line naming a closed family · guard: issue_gate.py
  17. Send the TfNSW bespoke-table request (mode by age, trip length distribution by mode, occupancy by purpose). data repeat ×9
    walk's distribution, ride's who-drives, and car trip time by purpose are each a mode's missing observation.
    change: cities/newcastle/docs/requests/tfnsw_hts_bespoke_tables.md (drafted) - a user decision (D2) · guard: -
  18. verify_producer --against-committed runs HEAD's script from a temp copy instead of overwriting the tracked file. code
    build_manifest.py:1026 overwrites the working script; a crash mid-run silently reverts an in-flight edit.
    change: src/build/build_manifest.py · guard: test_verify_producer
  19. A PreToolUse hook that refuses the shell stdin trap (heredoc, bare python -). process
    hit by this session and by three of its agents; stated as advice only.
    change: .claude/hooks/ · guard: the hook
  20. Run the built ASC fixed-point loop once the mechanisms above land, as Berlin v6 did (mode-share error under 0.5 pp in ~5 runs). model
    asc_fixed_point.py is built and held; constants absorb what mechanisms cannot, and must be solved last and measured against a band.
    change: src/calibrate/asc_fixed_point.py; a lane decision · guard: replication band

Appendix

Rubric: 5 exemplary, 4 sound with minor gaps, 3 workable with named defects, 2 materially deficient, 1 failing its purpose. Search rounds: field 24 of 40 calls over 5 rounds; factors 19 of 40. Reproduce: the three collectors, then render_report.py over the lanes' JSON.
